# <b><c1/></b>: RINEX clock files, versions { "2" } and { "3.00" } to { "3.04" } (including the { "3.04" } nine-character name field). The header is read only for its <c2/> and is skipped up to <c3/>. Of the data records, only the <c4/> (satellite) and <c5/> (receiver or station) records of one clock, selected by name, are kept. Their first value field, the clock bias in seconds, is the phase. Continuation lines and the <c6/>, <c7/> and <c8/> records are skipped. Epochs are the record's calendar fields, as seconds from 1980-01-06 { "00" }:{ "00" }:{ "00" } in the file's own time system, with no conversion.

# c3: END OF HEADER

# c5: AR
# c6: CR
# c7: DR

La cabecera se lee solo por su <c2/> y se omite hasta <c3/>. De los registros de datos, solo se conservan los registros <c4/> (satélite) y <c5/> (receptor o estación) de un reloj, seleccionado por nombre. # Kshana Studio, Simple view, on the bundled scenario integrity-raim, engine { "v0.38.0" } running locally. # How a run flows. A scenario TOML file (a kind, a seed and its parameters) goes into the engine, kshana { $version }, through the api::run_toml dispatch over { "87" } scenario kinds, deterministic from scenario, seed and engine version. The engine writes result.json, chart.svg, report.html and report.json, a table.csv for the kinds that define one, and on request SP3, CCSDS OMM and OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK and SigMF exports; a suite writes study.json and study.html. Those files feed Kshana Studio in the browser, an AI assistant through the kshana-mcp server, and continuous integration.
# attr:alt
seg-76e3ec458441 = Cómo fluye una ejecución. Un archivo TOML de escenario (un tipo de escenario, una semilla y sus parámetros) entra en el motor, kshana { $version }, a través del despacho api::run_toml sobre { "87" } tipos de escenario, determinista a partir del escenario, la semilla y la versión del motor. El motor escribe result.json, chart.svg, report.html y report.json, un table.csv para los tipos de escenario que lo definen y, bajo petición, exportaciones SP3, CCSDS OMM y OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK y SigMF; una suite escribe study.json y study.html. Esos archivos alimentan Kshana Studio en el navegador, a un asistente de IA a través del servidor kshana-mcp y la integración continua.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check, and the label that follows. Continuous integration (CI) makes it impossible to call a capability VALIDATED without an independent external oracle.
# text
seg-707e2cf795f6 = <b>{ "176" } de { "388" }</b> capacidades validadas frente a referencias independientes externas; { "208" } etiquetadas con honestidad como modeladas. Cada fila de la matriz de verificación nombra una capacidad, la referencia independiente frente a la que se contrasta, la prueba que ejecuta la comprobación y la etiqueta que se deriva. La integración continua (CI) hace imposible llamar VALIDATED a una capacidad sin una referencia independiente externa.

# How a capability earns its label. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check in continuous integration, and the label that follows. A row may be VALIDATED only with an independent external oracle; otherwise it is MODELLED, or PARTNER when a hardware partner owns it. Example: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + perturbations, batch/sequential OD, oracle AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4 verification vectors, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, label VALIDATED. Live counts: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER of { "388" } rows. In all, { "176" } capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } more are honestly labelled MODELLED and { "4" } are PARTNER-owned.
# attr:alt
seg-2f81cf391eae = Cómo gana una capacidad su etiqueta. Cada fila de la matriz de verificación nombra una capacidad, la referencia independiente frente a la que se contrasta, la prueba que ejecuta la comprobación en integración continua y la etiqueta que se deriva. Una fila solo puede ser VALIDATED con una referencia independiente externa; si no, es MODELLED, o PARTNER cuando la posee un socio de hardware. Ejemplo: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + perturbaciones, OD por lotes/secuencial, referencia independiente vectores de verificación SGP4 de AIAA 2006-6753, prueba tests/sgp4_verification.rs, etiqueta VALIDATED. Recuentos en vivo: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER de { "388" } filas. En total, { "176" } capacidades validadas frente a referencias independientes externas; otras { "208" } están etiquetadas con honestidad como MODELLED y { "4" } son propiedad de un PARTNER.

# Kshana's architecture: one open engine at the centre, kshana { $version } under the AGPL-3.0, with api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over every kind, and the verification ledger of { "388" } capabilities ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Around it, every surface runs the same engine: Command line (cargo install kshana); Rust library (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker image (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains plugin (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Below it, Kshana Pro, a proprietary overlay that depends on the open engine as a library and never forks it, and adds no physical model.
# attr:alt
seg-9d69cf43cd40 = Arquitectura de Kshana: un motor abierto en el centro, kshana { $version } bajo la AGPL-3.0, con api::run_toml, un despacho tipado sobre todos los tipos de escenario, y el registro de verificación de { "388" } capacidades ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). A su alrededor, cada superficie ejecuta el mismo motor: línea de comandos (cargo install kshana); biblioteca de Rust (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); servidor MCP (cargo install kshana-mcp); imagen de Docker (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); complemento de JetBrains (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Por debajo, Kshana Pro, una capa propietaria que depende del motor abierto como biblioteca, nunca lo bifurca y no añade ningún modelo físico.

# Kshana's architecture in layers. Five front doors (command line, Python, JavaScript and WebAssembly, the MCP server and the JetBrains plugin) reach one api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over { "87" } kinds. Beneath it sit eight domain layers: time and frames, clocks and timing, inertial and fusion, GNSS and integrity, astrodynamics, LEO PNT, Moon, Mars and deep space, and missions and studies, on a shared core, all cross-referenced by the verification module, the machine-checked matrix of { "388" } capabilities that is the single source of truth for every label.
# attr:alt
seg-9bc9a971bdb4 = Arquitectura de Kshana por capas. Por debajo hay ocho capas de dominio: tiempo y marcos, relojes y temporización, inercial y fusión, GNSS e integridad, astrodinámica, PNT LEO, Luna, Marte y espacio profundo, y misiones y estudios, sobre un núcleo compartido, todas con referencias cruzadas del módulo de verificación, la matriz comprobada por máquina de { "388" } capacidades que es la única fuente de verdad de cada etiqueta.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled, { "4" } partner-owned. Each capability carries one label in a machine-checked ledger: VALIDATED (an independent external oracle agrees: real data, an independent implementation or published reference vectors), MODELLED (internally consistent, and said out loud) or PARTNER (a hardware partner owns it). Among the checks: all { "666" } SGP4 (Simplified General Perturbations { "4" }) vectors of AIAA (American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics) 2006-6753 to { "4.12" } mm, and the Cowell force model to { "0.08" } m against Orekit { "12.2" }.
# text
seg-2d2c47db0c1f = <b>{ "176" } de { "388" }</b> capacidades validadas frente a referencias independientes externas; { "208" } etiquetadas con honestidad como modeladas, { "4" } propiedad de un socio. Cada capacidad lleva una etiqueta en un registro comprobado por máquina: VALIDATED (concuerda una referencia independiente externa: datos reales, una implementación independiente o vectores de referencia publicados), MODELLED (internamente coherente, y declarado en voz alta) o PARTNER (la posee un socio de hardware). Entre las comprobaciones: los { "666" } vectores SGP4 (Simplified General Perturbations { "4" }) de AIAA (American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics) 2006-6753 hasta { "4.12" } mm, y el modelo de fuerzas de Cowell hasta { "0.08" } m frente a Orekit { "12.2" }.

# Three independent tools, pre-registered (0123cee1) before any fixture or oracle output existed, tolerances fixed then. ({ "1" }) ARAIM: Stanford MAAST for ARAIM { "2" } (commit ab70e2a3, BSD-3) mhss_raim_baseline_v5.m, run as a separate program under GNU Octave { "8.4" } with a driver-side unique() compatibility shim (disclosed, no algorithm change), at the ADD TOL_PL of { "0.05" } m: the Rust reproduces the ADD reference implementation on { "270" } real cases. Disclosed: the Kshana ADD path was written after the pre-registration, following the ADD as MAAST implements it; an independent hold-out by the reviewer ({ "270" } new user geometries and a second integrity support message, MAAST re-run against the frozen code) passed at the same bar. ({ "2" }) Snapshot RAIM: RTKLIB v2.4.2-p13 (BSD-2) estpos/valsol/raim_fde in a separate C harness for detection and exclusion; the slope PL from RTKLIB matinv/xyz2enu plus SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2, where both sides evaluate the same closed form (Brown; Parkinson and Axelrad), so the PL check covers the implementation, not the formula. ({ "3" }) SBAS: Stanford MAAST (commit 7d32b049) usr_vhpl, unmodified, on MAAST own recorded WAAS broadcasts: L1 levels within { "1e-4" } m after the K rescaling and an equal protected set. The same comparisons produced the findings stated in the capability: the uniform-sigma ARAIM functions are not ADD-conformant, and the L5 set equality fails on { "308" } pairs with no GEO in view (the levels on the { "3267" } pairs both tools protect agree to { "1e-13" } m). The DO-229E/DO-316 K-factors are transcribed constants and the real geometry is an input; neither is counted as an oracle. { "0.30" } revision: snapshot_raim protection levels moved from the radial to the geodetic local level (up to { "0.31" } m HPL and { "0.14" } m VPL at { "16" } deg N)
# text
seg-a83c68500982 = Tres herramientas independientes, preregistradas (0123cee1) antes de que existiera cualquier dato de prueba o salida de referencia independiente, con las tolerancias fijadas entonces. ({ "1" }) ARAIM: Stanford MAAST para ARAIM { "2" } (commit ab70e2a3, BSD-3) mhss_raim_baseline_v5.m, ejecutado como programa aparte con GNU Octave { "8.4" } y un adaptador de compatibilidad de unique() en el controlador (declarado, sin cambio de algoritmo), con el TOL_PL de ADD de { "0.05" } m: el código Rust reproduce la implementación de referencia de ADD en { "270" } casos reales. Declarado: la ruta de ADD de Kshana se escribió después del preregistro, siguiendo ADD tal como lo implementa MAAST; una reserva independiente del revisor ({ "270" } geometrías de usuario nuevas y un segundo mensaje de soporte de integridad, con MAAST ejecutado de nuevo contra el código congelado) se superó con el mismo umbral. ({ "2" }) RAIM instantáneo: RTKLIB v2.4.2-p13 (BSD-2) estpos/valsol/raim_fde en un arnés de C aparte para detección y exclusión; el PL por pendiente a partir de matinv/xyz2enu de RTKLIB más chi2/ncx2 de SciPy { "1.18.1" }, donde ambos lados evalúan la misma forma cerrada (Brown; Parkinson y Axelrad), de modo que la comprobación del PL cubre la implementación, no la fórmula. ({ "3" }) SBAS: Stanford MAAST (commit 7d32b049) usr_vhpl, sin modificar, sobre las propias difusiones WAAS grabadas de MAAST: niveles de L1 dentro de { "1e-4" } m tras el reescalado de K y un conjunto protegido igual. Las mismas comparaciones produjeron los hallazgos indicados en la capacidad: las funciones de ARAIM de sigma uniforme no se ajustan a ADD, y la igualdad de conjuntos de L5 falla en { "308" } pares sin GEO a la vista (los niveles de los { "3267" } pares que ambas herramientas protegen coinciden hasta { "1e-13" } m). Los factores K de DO-229E/DO-316 son constantes transcritas y la geometría real es una entrada; ninguno se cuenta como referencia independiente. Revisión { "0.30" }: los niveles de protección de snapshot_raim pasaron del nivel local radial al geodésico (hasta { "0.31" } m de HPL y { "0.14" } m de VPL a { "16" } deg N)

# RTKLIB v2.4.2-p13 (T. Takasu, BSD-2-Clause, commit 71db0ffa) geph2clk and geph2pos, compiled from C source in a separate harness (xval/rtklib-glonass) that reads the same RINEX file with RTKLIB's own reader, so the parse, the sign of -tau_n, the units and the iteration are compared as well as the clock and the orbit. Pre-registered e099c42a before the function existed and before the harness was built. Both sides evaluate the same clock closed form, so the clock comparison covers the implementation, not the formula. The orbit integrators differ (RTKLIB steps { "60" } s then the remainder, Kshana equal steps of at most { "60" } s) and so do the constants (RTKLIB mu { "3.9860044e14" }, Kshana the PZ-90.11 { "3.986004418e14" }); the { "1.1" } mm worst case is that difference, as predicted before the run. Scope: the broadcast ephemeris within its validity interval, not the true orbit or clock (no comparison with precise products)

Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. # Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision

({ "1" }) Viaje de ida y vuelta en forma cerrada: el sesgo mínimo detectable se produce invirtiendo la cola del chi cuadrado no central sobre el parámetro de no centralidad (raim::pbias) y se verifica devolviendo el parámetro de no centralidad resultante por raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf en el umbral propio del monitor, que debe devolver P_md. ({ "2" }) Inyección frente a análisis: se escribe un sesgo en la estimación de RF de un eje y se vuelve a ejecutar cross_raim::run_cross_raim, de modo que el estadístico comparado con el parámetro de no centralidad analítico es el que calculó el monitor, no una rederivación. Un Monte Carlo con semilla del propio estadístico se lleva en las pruebas como tercera comprobación; deliberadamente NO está en el informe, porque una estimación muestreada sería más lenta y no reproducible bit a bit. # An independent in-repo route. Every Jacobian row is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s evaluated from the state's own Earth-fixed and Moon-body-fixed coordinates rotated forward through the frame chain — a path that computes no derivative and shares no expression with the analytic partials — and the information matrices the two routes build agree to < { "1e-6" } of the largest diagonal. The linear-algebra kernel this composes (information_matrix, crlb, sym_eig) is separately externally anchored against numpy.linalg.eigh / numpy.linalg.inv in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs, and the delay observable carries lunar_vlbi's own delta-DOR far-field oracle; the row does not borrow either status. Closed-form identities checked here and labelled as such: σ² and 1/N scaling, and √(p/trace(M)) as an AM-HM lower bound the computed sigma cannot beat, which coincides with the equipartition value only on an isotropic geometry — which is why the measured ratio is exactly the anisotropy the assumption discarded. { "0.30" } external comparison (Library: NAIF SPICE CSPICE N0067 via spiceypy { "8.2.0" }, MIT; DE440, ITRF93 earth_latest_high_prec, MOON_PA), pre-registered ({ "50863551" }) together with an engine-side model-error sensitivity run: every Jacobian row is rebuilt by central differences of converged Newtonian light-time differences from SPICE geometry (the light-time equation is a short script over SPICE positions; SPICE carries the geometry), with the schedule rebuilt from SPICE elevations. The schedule, rank, defect, information spectrum, condition number, station sigmas, headline, trace bound, equipartition ratios, free-network null space, line-of-sight sweep and beacon declination agree within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.1" } deg, which sits above the engine known analytic-Moon error ({ "3.2e-3" } on sigmas, measured before the run). NumPy { "2.3.5" } redoes the linear algebra on the committed engine Jacobian to { "2e-13" } (P2). Validated as a Cramér-Rao bound for the stated reduced parameter set on the stated illustrative inputs: the Moon-centre ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held fixed in both legs, so it is not a real-campaign accuracy

# Internal consistency: the brute-force elevation scan, the hand-computed and orbit::dop reductions, and the closed-form Walker and geosynchronous geometry. Two-body orbits with an optional secular J2, a spherical body with the local vertical along the radius, geometric visibility only (no signal power, satellite health, terrain or third-body perturbation), and the relative phase between systems taken from different reference epochs, so it is not a snapshot of any date. { "0.30" } review, a finding (stays MODELLED): the presets meet the published one-sided DOP-availability floors (BeiDou OS Performance Standard { "3.0" }, Galileo OS SDD) at { "100" } %, but with the engine's PDOP doubled in a scratch copy they still meet them (worst point { "99.36" } %), so the floors cannot detect even a { "100" } % error in the quantity this row computes; tightening them after the run would be a post-hoc tolerance. Promotion needs a two-sided comparison of DOP values on identical geometry with a pre-set tolerance. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (tests/coverage_dop_full_oracle.rs, pre-registered add671d6), a finding (stays MODELLED): Orekit { "12.2" } positions, elevation, azimuth and ground tracks with gnss_lib_py { "1.0.4" } GDOP/PDOP/HDOP/VDOP (common clock) and a numpy inverse (one clock per constellation) agree on every bar on the Galileo, GPS, BeiDou, Galileo+GPS (one clock each) and a 1584-satellite Earth design (DOP within { "2e-14" } relative, identical fix, availability and visibility counts). On the Moon and Mars designs the elements, positions, visibility and tracks agree, but DOP exceeds the pre-set { "1e-9" } relative bar in cells whose largest PDOP is { "1434" } or more (up to { "8e-6" }), and { "4" } cells differ by one fix epoch on numerically singular geometry (engine pivot { "1e-10" } against numpy rank), so the row is not validated. { "0.30" } revisions: the TDOP/GDOP reference clock is now the lowest-numbered constellation in view (constellation-multi-gnss-coverage global GDOP max { "2.2236" } -> { "2.1205" }, mean { "1.122" } -> { "1.131" }), and per-constellation visible counts are summed as integers

Órbitas de dos cuerpos con J2 secular opcional, un cuerpo esférico con la vertical local a lo largo del radio, solo visibilidad geométrica (sin potencia de señal, salud del satélite, terreno ni perturbación de terceros cuerpos), y la fase relativa entre sistemas tomada de épocas de referencia diferentes, por lo que no es una instantánea de ninguna fecha. { "0.30" } revisión, un hallazgo (sigue siendo MODELLED): los preajustes alcanzan los suelos unilaterales publicados de disponibilidad de DOP (BeiDou OS Performance Standard { "3.0" }, Galileo OS SDD) al { "100" } %, pero con el PDOP del motor duplicado en una copia de trabajo siguen alcanzándolos (peor punto { "99.36" } %), de modo que los suelos no pueden detectar ni siquiera un error del { "100" } % en la magnitud que calcula esta fila; endurecerlos después de la ejecución sería una tolerancia a posteriori. # Real precise science orbits as truth (TU Graz IfG/ITSG operational reduced-dynamic orbits, free for any use with acknowledgment; SHA-256 values in the fixture NOTICE) fitted by the 16-parameter set over { "20" } and { "30" } min arcs, against the statistics Liu et al. { "2025" } (Remote Sensing { "17" }({ "16" }):{ "2894" }, CC BY { "4.0" }) publish for the same satellites and days: along, cross and radial RMS each within 1.5x (measured { "0.804" } to { "1.087" }), SISRE within 1.5x (measured { "0.716" } to { "0.809" }) and SISRE weights within { "0.005" } of its Table { "2" } (measured { "0.0007" }); tolerances fixed before the comparison. The per-component gate gives the test its teeth: zeroing the inclination rate leaves every SISRE ratio inside 1.5x but fails the cross-track gate. The uniform { "0.72" } to { "0.81" } SISRE ratio is the paper's: its printed SISRE values use Table { "2" }'s weights unsquared rather than its own Eq. { "9" }, which Kshana follows (Montenbruck et al. { "2018" }). The integrated truth orbit (zonal J2-J6, EGM2008 to degree { "20" } and { "70" }, drag) agrees with Orekit { "12.2" } (CS GROUP, Apache-2.0) and Hipparchus { "3.1" }, propagated from the same initial state in the same frames with the same density input, within { "4.55" } mm over { "6" } h (bar { "0.02" } m fixed before the run, pre-registration 65e4d46c). Outside the claim: the correction polynomials, the clock fit and the update-period trade, which keep internal checks (a two-body truth recovered by the 16-parameter set to below { "1" } mm, and an internal numpy cross-check of the exported messages on GRACE-FO with its CLK1B clock, which was written and run by this project and is not an independent oracle)

Measured: datum sigmas within { "3.0e-13" } on 2026-06-09 (bars { "4.1e-7" } to { "5.3e-7" }), { "1.9e-15" } with the stations fixed (bars down to { "4.8e-12" }), { "3.8e-13" } on 2026-03-18 and { "2.3e-13" } on 2024-01-01, where the default spectral solver is off by { "4.8e-6" } and { "9.7e-6" }; condition number within { "4.9e-13" }; weakest direction within { "1.4e-13" } rad (control { "1.7e-16" } against { "5.1e-10" }). LIMIT OF THE CHECK: the bars certify only datum errors below about { "2e-7" } relative; the measured { "1e-13" } figures are observations, not what the bars certify. The check rejects the default spectral solver (sigmas { "9.7e-6" } against { "2.9e-7" } on 2024-01-01; on the binding date { "1.3e-6" } against { "4.9e-7" }, a margin of { "1.3" } to { "2.6" }) and a mis-taken triangular inverse (column norms: red), but a flipped Householder sign, an uncompensated dot product, and sigmas from a spectral inverse of the H formed from the square-root factor ({ "3.7e-9" }) all pass, so it does not show that each square-root step is needed. Validates the opt-in solver's linear algebra on committed inputs, not the Jacobian and not the default solver; bit-reproducibility across platforms is argued from the operations used and tested only as rerun identity on one platform

Schema { "1.3" } ({ "0.36" }), pre-registered (fb226057) before any tool read it: for a 64-line synthetic schema-1.3 stream (geometry statuses, DOPs, labels and signal loss present, null, absent and unknown; deduction classes; unparsable lines) the same two parsers read the geometry series (Prometheus kshana_trust_geometry*, OTLP kshana.geometry.*) and the class label on kshana_trust_reason_active exactly as the registry implies ({ "24" } families and { "54" } samples; { "12" } metrics and { "31" } points), promtool { "3.5.0" } check metrics exits { "0" } with no lint problem, and the OpenTelemetry Collector contrib { "0.135.0" } accepts the OTLP/HTTP JSON (status { "200" }, nothing rejected) and its file exporter records the request unchanged (xval/telemetry-1.3, data-gated: the release tarballs are fetched with pinned SHA-256 and their outputs committed). WHAT IS NOT: the HTTP endpoint, delivery, timeouts and reconnection are not checked by an oracle, nor the Collector's prometheus receiver; the registry is read through accessors, so a fault in the registry state itself would not be caught; the OTLP 'committed output is current' check runs only with the otlp feature; the metric names and their usefulness to an operations centre are not validated; CEF and LEEF are not covered (see the next row).

# WHAT IS EXTERNALLY CHECKED, stated narrowly: against two independent open-source parsers (oracles: pycef { "1.11" } (PyPI, MIT); the Logstash CEF codec (logstash-codec-cef { "6.2.7" } in Logstash { "8.15.3" })), on a corpus of CEF lines this crate wrote ({ "157" } before amendment { "4" } below, { "170" } after it, { "176" } after amendment { "5" }): every band change and every epoch of a committed 84-line synthetic trust stream, plus adversarial text in hosts, reasons, time labels and gate names containing equals signs, backslashes, backslash-equals, pipes, line feeds, carriage returns, tabs, runs of spaces, multi-byte and astral characters and key lookalikes, extreme scores and offsets, every band with and without a previous band. pycef parses the lines whose header needs no escaping ({ "153" } before amendment { "4" }, { "166" } after it, { "172" } after amendment { "5" }) and recovers, exactly, the header fields, the severity, the exact extension key set and every value (numbers by f64 equality), after the CEF specification's unescape rules, which pycef does not apply and which scripts/gen_cef_ref.py applies. The Logstash codec, which unescapes by itself, parses every line ({ "157" }, then { "170" }, then { "176" }), meets the same bar, and also recovers four header fields that need escaping (a pipe, a backslash and an equals sign in the version string), which pycef's header pattern cannot read, so header escaping rests on the Logstash codec alone. The comparison bar was registered in commits { "5172e579" } (pycef) and 0a7598aa (Logstash) before either parser was run, and both met it at the first comparison. Five amendments are disclosed. Amendments ({ "1" }) to ({ "3" }) touched no comparison rule and no expected value: ({ "1" }) a corpus-size assertion was corrected from { "5" } to { "4" } band changes, a miscount, since the stream has { "4" }; ({ "2" }) the Logstash runner's pipeline delimiter wiring was fixed after a first attempt that produced no events; ({ "3" }) the trust score and epoch offset were moved from the CEF integer fields cn1 and cn2 to the floating-point fields cfp1 and cfp2, a wire-format change made in this release, after which the corpus was regenerated and both oracles were re-run with the bar unchanged and met it again. Amendment ({ "4" }), { "0.36" }, extended the corpus and the expected key set, registered in d6331429 before either parser ran on the new lines: schema { "1.3" } inputs add the satellite geometry in the custom fields cs6 (label geometry: status, then ';' and the deduction classes), cfp3 (geometryHdop) and cfp4 (geometryPdop); { "13" } geometry cases (the corpus to { "170" } lines) and the three new keys; both met the bar, otherwise unchanged, at the first comparison (pycef { "166" } of { "170" } lines parsed, the { "4" } tier { "2" } header cases as before; Logstash { "170" } of { "170" }). Amendment ({ "5" }), { "0.36" }, appended { "6" } geometry-label cases (the corpus to { "176" } lines), registered in 9a8e4fa9 before either parser ran on them: from inputs that carry the geometry label, cs6 gains a third field (';' and the label) and cn1 (geometryUsedSatellites) the satellites used; the Logstash codec's mapping table was read first (no flexString fields, hence cs6 rather than flexString1); both met the bar at the first comparison (pycef { "172" } of { "176" }, Logstash { "176" } of { "176" }), and every earlier reference entry was reproduced unchanged. WHAT IS NOT: neither parser type-checks the cfp values (both keep them as text), so the use of the floating-point fields rests on the CEF dictionary rather than on an oracle; no vendor SIEM was run; the extension key names and their mapping are this crate's choice, and the parsers show only that they are read back as written; the RFC { "5424" } envelope, delivery, timeouts and reconnection are not covered; pycef is a small parser last released in { "2020" }, and the Logstash codec is the more substantial oracle.

Con las estaciones estimadas difiere en { "6.8e-6" } frente a { "1e-9" } preregistrado, cuya premisa del número de condición era errónea (condición de Helmert { "2.1e8" }; las vías independientes de NumPy discrepan en { "3e-6" }; no se descarta una contribución del respaldo de pseudoinversa { "1e-9" } del motor), por lo que la fila no se promueve con ese preregistro. { "0.36" } nuevo registro con un umbral escalado por la condición (preregistrado b887eb05; t = max({ "1e-12" }, { "10" } kappa { "2" }^-53) a partir del condicionamiento de la información conjunta y su reducción de Helmert), un hallazgo (se mantiene MODELLED, NOT MET): el paso { "0" } DESCARTA el respaldo de pseudoinversa para la brecha de { "6.8e-6" } (ninguna matriz anterior a la matriz de Helmert recurre al respaldo en los seis escenarios originales, con un margen mínimo de un factor { "2.6" }), el tramo de SPICE sigue cumpliéndose en los seis, y NumPy coincide dentro del umbral en seis de ocho escenarios (por defecto, los dos arcos, el sigma de retardo 3x, el defecto colineal y la nueva ejecución con estaciones fijas del 2026-12-01; peor caso { "0.13" } de su umbral). Falla en stations_estimated (sigmas de datum hasta { "6.8e-6" }, condición { "9.1e-6" }, norma con independencia descartada { "3.5e-6" }, frente a t = { "5.9e-7" }), y en la fecha nueva la baliza del polo sur no tiene ninguna observación, de modo que la información de la baliza es de rango deficiente (respaldo de pseudoinversa anterior a la matriz de Helmert en ambos escenarios nuevos) y la referencia independiente NumPy sin cambios no puede evaluar stations_estimated_fresh (matriz singular). seg-ce6d0ecab448 = El servidor es un <b>crate independiente, excluido del workspace</b>, con su propio <c1/>, de modo que el SDK (kit de desarrollo de software) oficial de MCP para Rust, <c2/>, y su entorno de ejecución asíncrono nunca entran en el grafo de dependencias del crate <c3/> publicado: un usuario de la biblioteca o de Python no compila un servidor que no ejecuta. (Se separó por primera vez porque <c4/> es <b>edición { "2024" }</b> y necesita rustc ≥ { "1.85" }, mientras que el crate principal prometía entonces { "1.75" }. La biblioteca declara ahora { "1.85" }; este servidor declara { "1.88" }, el mínimo de <c5/> { "2" }.x con parches de seguridad.) La integración continua (CI) controla este crate por separado: pruebas y <c6/> frente a su archivo de bloqueo. El árbol de licencias de rmcp está limpio (Apache-2.0/MIT/BSD), así que, a diferencia del crate de validación cruzada <c7/>, que por lo demás imita, la separación no obedece a la licencia.

# checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. Upgrading this row to Validated needs a measured transmit pattern and LuGRE normal points to check against; neither is in the repository and neither is invented here. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, a finding (stays MODELLED): a measured-input link path (real SP3 positions, per-block transmit power, tabulated transmit and receive patterns, acquisition and tracking hysteresis) was checked against Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Table { "3" } for GENESIS at { "6000" } km (pre-registered 5b414f0a): zenith-antenna means agree on { "5" } of { "6" } within one dB of the paper sensitivity; nadir-antenna means are { "1.0" } to { "2.4" } satellites higher than printed, plausibly because the paper used azimuth-dependent GPS side-lobe patterns that are not published. The lunar-distance figures of this row still use the Airy stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, a finding (stays MODELLED): the restated quantity, the C/N0 DIFFERENCE between Block IIR/IIR-M satellites tracked at one epoch, predicted with the NAVCEN per-SVN measured L1 patterns (two-dimensional, yaw-steering frame) against the LuGRE flight C/N0 (pre-registered 768cb62b: { "99" } % visible, pair RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, at least { "50" } records and { "30" } pairs) has too little data: the { "26" } qualifying transit and lunar-orbit epochs hold { "15" } tracked IIR/IIR-M records (mostly IIF and III are tracked) and one pair. # checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision
# text
seg-88d196f13afa = comprobado frente a su propia identidad analítica o de forma cerrada: detecta errores de transcripción y de coeficientes, pero no es una referencia independiente externa — Dos referencias independientes internas, ningún conjunto de datos externo. ({ "1" }) Viaje de ida y vuelta de forma cerrada: el sesgo mínimo detectable se obtiene invirtiendo la cola de la chi-cuadrado no central en el parámetro de no centralidad (raim::pbias) y se verifica devolviendo la no centralidad resultante a raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf en el umbral propio del monitor, que debe devolver P_md. ({ "2" }) Inyección frente a análisis: se escribe un sesgo en la estimación RF de un eje y se vuelve a ejecutar cross_raim::run_cross_raim, de modo que el estadístico comparado con la no centralidad analítica es el que calculó el monitor, no una nueva derivación. Una simulación de Monte Carlo con semilla del propio estadístico se incluye en las pruebas como tercera comprobación; deliberadamente NO figura en el informe, porque una estimación muestreada sería más lenta y no reproducible bit a bit. ExternalDataset se descarta: la magnitud es la potencia de detección de ESTE monitor con la asignación de sigma de ESTE escenario, y las magnitudes de sigma por las que se escala el MDB son entradas representativas modeladas, no mediciones. ReferenceImpl también se consideró y se descartó: la simulación de Monte Carlo muestrea el mismo estadístico que describe el análisis, por lo que detecta errores de transcripción y de coeficientes, pero no es una implementación independiente del monitor. { "0.30" } ronda { "2" } (preregistrada 0135a9e4), bloqueada (sigue siendo MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproducen el umbral del informe (con una diferencia de { "5.4e-12" }), el MDB por eje, la curva de potencia, el múltiplo de cruce y el tiempo de detección de rampa (con una diferencia de { "5.3e-10" }) en cinco configuraciones; las estadísticas de fallos inyectados carecen de referencia independiente y las magnitudes de sigma son entradas modeladas, por lo que la delimitación del alcance queda a la espera de una decisión del responsable

({ "1" }) Una señal analítica con coeficientes conocidos: la única forma de ejercitar la maquinaria periódica, ya que ninguna serie incluida es lo bastante larga para admitir un término anual. ({ "2" }) La solución de mínimos cuadrados de forma cerrada de los manuales, con un álgebra distinta de la resolución matricial sometida a prueba, sobre filas reales. # Not yet on GitHub
seg-f942b36a11c4 = Aún no está en GitHub

# <b>What the observatory does instead.</b> The question the almanac comparison answers is "could the constellation geometry alone explain this low accuracy?". The base method answers a related question without an almanac: a low share must stand out against the same day's background across the region and against the aircraft's own good reports elsewhere. That is a different and weaker substitute: it cannot say that a quiet-geometry hour was degraded where the day's background is also low. An almanac-HDOP scorer is <b>not implemented</b>. It is an open item, to be built from the paper's full text and the public GPS almanac and pre-registered before it is run; until then it is cited, not claimed.
# text
seg-4f8471649a07 = <b>Lo que hace el observatorio en su lugar.</b> La pregunta a la que responde la comparación con el almanaque es «¿podría la sola geometría de la constelación explicar esta baja exactitud?». El método base responde a una pregunta relacionada sin almanaque: una proporción baja debe destacar frente al fondo del mismo día en toda la región y frente a los buenos informes de la propia aeronave en otros lugares. Es un sustituto distinto y más débil: no puede decir que una hora de geometría tranquila estuvo degradada allí donde el fondo del día también es bajo. Un evaluador de HDOP de almanaque <b>no está implementado</b>. Es un asunto abierto, que se construirá a partir del texto completo del artículo y del almanaque público de GPS y se preregistrará antes de ejecutarse; hasta entonces se cita, no se afirma.
